--- Page 3 ---
<meta>This page appears to be a title page or a frontispiece. The transcription notes indicate the text is very faint, likely a "ghost image" or offset where ink from an opposing page transferred over time.</meta>

->THE<-

->LIMITS<-

->OF<-

->NATURAL<-

->RELIGION<-

<note>original: "Les Bornes de la Religion Naturelle"</note>

<term>Natural Religion: A system of belief based on human reason and the observation of the physical world, rather than on supernatural revelation or organized church doctrine. This was a central topic of debate during the Enlightenment.</term>

<term>Limits: In this context, the word refers to the boundaries or extent to which reason can understand the divine before it must yield to faith or mystery.</term>

<summary>This page serves as the title for a philosophical or theological treatise concerning "Natural Religion," a popular concept in 18th and 19th-century thought.</summary>
<keywords>Natural Religion, Enlightenment philosophy, limits of reason, theology, boundaries of faith</keywords>

<summary>This is the title page for a religious treatise written by C.M.I.B. Danne, a former Protestant minister who converted to Catholicism and became a legal advisor to the Duke of Savoy. The work is specifically addressed to his former congregation in the Luserna Valley, a region known for its Waldensian (Protestant) population.</summary>

<summary>This title page introduces a religious defense of Catholicism written by Claude-Maure-Bruno Danne, a former Protestant minister targeting his former congregation in the Waldensian valleys. --- Page 9 ---
<meta>This page is the formal dedication of the 1689 treatise "The Pillars of the Catholic Religion," following the title page translated previously. The author, Claude-Maure-Bruno Danne, addresses his patron, Victor Amadeus II, the Duke of Savoy, framing his work within a tradition of legacy and administrative duty, much like the Roman Emperor Augustus.</meta>

<sig>A 3</sig>

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut headpiece featuring symmetrical scrollwork, foliage, and a central human face mask.</image-desc>

### TO
## HIS ROYAL HIGHNESS
# VICTOR
# AMADEUS II.
### Duke of Savoy, Prince of Piedmont, King of Cyprus, &c. <note>Victor Amadeus II (1666–1732) was the Duke of Savoy and would later become King of Sicily and then Sardinia. The title "King of Cyprus" was a prestige title claimed by the House of Savoy through the inheritance of the Lusignan dynasty.</note>

## *MY LORD.*

<image-desc>An ornamental historiated initial letter 'L' featuring floral motifs within a square border.</image-desc>

HISTORY teaches us that Caesar Augustus, being on his deathbed, had Drusus <note>This refers to Drusus the Younger, the son of the Emperor Tiberius.</note>, the son of Tiberius, read four Registers <note>These "Registers" refer to the *Res Gestae Divi Augusti* and other administrative summaries Augustus left to his successors to guide the management of the Roman Empire.</note> that he had prepared to serve...

<summary>This page contains the formal dedication of the work to Victor Amadeus II, Duke of Savoy. The author, Danne, opens with a classical allusion to Caesar Augustus to emphasize the importance of leaving a documented legacy or "register" for a sovereign.</summary>

<keywords>Victor Amadeus II, Duke of Savoy, dedication, Caesar Augustus, Drusus, House of Savoy, Roman history, political legacy</keywords>

--- Page 10 ---
<meta>This page continues the formal dedication to Victor Amadeus II, Duke of Savoy. The previous page established a parallel between the Duke and the Roman Emperor Augustus, who left behind administrative "Registers" for his successors. The catchword "appel-" noted in the metadata indicates the first word of the following page.</meta>

...of instruction after his death; and the second contained his finest deeds, <margin>Roman History, vol. 1, p. 484.</margin> which he commanded to be engraved upon square columns made of bronze, wishing by this means to recommend to posterity an immortal memory of them. <note>This refers to the *Res Gestae Divi Augusti* ("The Deeds of the Divine Augustus"), an autobiographical record which Augustus ordered to be inscribed on bronze pillars in front of his mausoleum in Rome.</note>

*Our Great* JESUS *is the Emperor of the Universe who had recorded in His* Word *what He has done for men, and willed that His truths be engraved upon the* Bronze Column *of perpetual duration, namely the Church, which* <margin>1 Timothy 3:15.</margin> *St. Paul calls the* Pillar and Bulwark <note>The original French uses "Firmament," following the Latin "firmamentum" (a support or foundation), often translated in English Bibles as "ground" or "stay."</note> of Truth.

<margin>Matthew 16.</margin> *This adorable* SAVIOR *founded it upon* Peter <note>The author utilizes a linguistic pun: in French, "Pierre" is both the name "Peter" and the word for "stone" or "rock," referencing Matthew 16:18, "You are Peter, and on this rock I will build my church."</note>, *so that it might be* <margin>Matthew 7.</margin> *like the* Wise Man's House, *unshakable against all the shocks of the opposing forces that he has*

<summary>Danne transitions from the historical legacy of Augustus to a theological argument, characterizing the Catholic Church as a permanent "Bronze Column" of truth established by Christ upon St. Peter.</summary>
<keywords>Augustus, Res Gestae, Jesus Christ, Catholic Church, St. Paul, St. Peter, Pillar of Truth, Biblical allegory, Roman history</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the formal dedication to Victor Amadeus II, Duke of Savoy. Having established the theological premise of his work—the "Twenty-Six Columns" of faith—the author now turns to the personal virtues and noble lineage of the Duke, employing the highly ornamental and flattering language typical of 17th-century courtly addresses.</meta>

<page-type>dedication</page-type>

If I look upon the blessings with which
Heaven has wished to crown *Y.R.H.* <note>Abbreviation for *Votre Altesse Royale* (Your Royal Highness), Victor Amadeus II.</note>,
my sight remains dazzled by their brilliance,
and my words know not how to ex-
press their greatness. You have the hon-
or of descending from the greatest
Princes who have ever existed under the Sun
in sovereignty of Dignity, Glory
of Virtue, and splendor of Piety.

<margin>Witnesses of Moral Philosophy <note>original Latin: *Tes. Phil. mor.*, likely referring to *Testimonia Philosophiae Moralis*.</note></margin>
And it is with good reason that Scholars
have called you the mirror and the col-
lection of the Virtues of the August,
the Magnanimous, the Invincible,
the Just, the Peaceful,
the Most Good and Most Great
Princes, your Predecessors.

And if I admire the qualities and
advantages possessed by the most
Glorious Monarchs spoken of by
History, I see none of them whose
perfect features <note>original: *lineamens*; the author suggests that the best traits of historical kings are visible in the Duke’s own character.</note> do not appear

<summary>The author praises Victor Amadeus II by situating him as the pinnacle of a noble lineage, possessing the gathered virtues of all great historical and "August" rulers. This section transitions from the book's theological defense to the personal glorification of its patron.</summary>

State, than to have all his domestic subjects of his Faith, imitators of his Piety, and partaking in the same graces. She <note>Likely referring to the Duchess Mother or the Princess consort.</note> spares neither wealth nor labor to bring to this duty those who are far from it, and Madame Royale <note>Original: *M. R.* (Madame Royale), the title held by Marie Jeanne Baptiste of Savoy, who acted as regent and was a prominent figure in the court.</note> employs her most charitable cares toward it.

RR.* (Vos Altesses Royales).</note> invite them to these joys with the tenderness of a Father and a Mother rather than a Prince and Princess; and if most of them do not respond to such cordial Kindness, it is because prejudice <note>The author uses the term "preoccupation" to describe the "pre-occupied" or biased minds of those (likely the Protestant Vaudois or Huguenots) resisting conversion to Catholicism.</note> does violence to their reason, and stops their ears from hearing the voice of God who calls them by such sweet and powerful means.

—nks to **GOD** who has drawn me from it by his power; and the affection that I bear for my **Country** obliging me to communicate to it the lights <note>In this 17th-century context, "lights" (French: *lumieres*) refers to spiritual insights or the divine truth that the author believes he has received upon his conversion.</note> he has granted me, I bring to it, according to the advice <margin>Titus 3:10</margin> <note>The Latin reference to Titus 3:10—"Warn a heretic first and then a second time; after that, have nothing to do with them"—serves as the scriptural basis for the author's "three warnings."</note> of **St. Paul**, three warnings regarding this. And so that it may be known to them and to their posterity, **Your Royal Highness** <note>Original: *V. A. R.* (*Votre Altesse Royale*). It serves them as an assurance of her most particular favors, if they would finally resolve to think seriously of their salvation, and to make themselves partakers of it through such a just duty.

I present this [work] at the **Feet** of **Your Royal Highness**, and beg **HER** to allow it to find a place under the shadow of her favor and protection. And after having implored her **Clemency** toward

--- Page 18 ---
<meta>This page contains the official ecclesiastical approvals for the publication, following the author's dedication to the House of Savoy. There is significant ink bleed-through from the reverse side of the paper.</meta>

We, the undersigned Theologians of the **Society of Jesus** <note>original: *Compagnie de IESVS*; the Jesuits</note>, certify that we have read a book entitled ***THE COLUMNS OF THE CATHOLIC RELIGION***, etc., in which we have found nothing that is not in conformity with the Catholic, Apostolic, and Roman Religion, and much that is useful for the instruction of those who have gone astray <note>original: *déuoyez*; likely referring to Protestants or those wandering from Catholic orthodoxy during the Counter-Reformation</note>. In witness whereof we have signed at Turin on July 20, 1679.

->**Alexandre Scot.**<-
->**Pierre Chappuis.**<-

*Having considered the aforementioned attestations, let it be printed* <note>original Latin: *Attentis præfatis Attestationibus Imprimatur*. An "Imprimatur" is an official license to print a book, ensuring it contains nothing contrary to faith or morals.</note>.
*Brother Michael Louis de Thevenard, Master of Sacred Theology, of the* **Order of Preachers** <note>original: *Ord. Præd.*, the Dominican Order, which historically oversaw the Office of the Inquisition</note>, *Inquisitor General of Turin, etc.*

<summary>This page presents the formal ecclesiastical approvals (approbations) for the book, signed by Jesuit theologians and the Inquisitor General of Turin in July 1679. These documents verify the work's doctrinal correctness and grant the legal "Imprimatur" required for publication in a Catholic state.</summary>
<keywords>approbation, Society of Jesus, Jesuits, Imprimatur, Inquisition, Turin, 1679, Michael Louis de Thevenard, Catholic orthodoxy, censors</keywords>

--- Page 20 ---
<meta>This page follows the Table of Contents and provides a list of errata—corrections for typographical and citation errors found within the main body of the text. This was a common feature in early modern publishing to ensure theological accuracy after the printing process.</meta>

# Errata

Page 10. *E*, read *Et* <note>original: "E" is an archaic or dialectical variant; "Et" is the standard French for "and"</note>; Page 10. *Petri Apostolorum*, read *Petri & Apostolorum* <note>original Latin: corrects "Peter of the Apostles" to "Peter and the Apostles"</note>; Page 11. *contr’elles*, read *entr’elles* <note>corrects "against them" to "between them"</note>; p. 14. *Confessions*, read *Confessions* <note>likely a correction of typeface or emphasis</note>; p. 16. *adherens*, read *adherans* <note>correction of the spelling for "adherents"</note>; p. 22. *confesione*, read *confensione* <note>original Latin: corrects "confession" to "consent" or "agreement"</note>; p. 29. *ét*, read *és* <note>correction to the archaic contraction for "in the"</note>; p. 32. *si*, read *sis* <note>corrects "if" to "situated"</note>; p. 33. *de*, read *de*; p. 45. *auiordhui*, read *auiourdhui* <note>corrects the spelling of the French word for "today"</note>; page 46. *on*, read *ont* <note>corrects the singular "one" to the plural "they have"</note>; p. 52. *accoûtumés*, read *accoûtumé* <note>corrects the plural to the singular "accustomed"</note>; p. 54. *souiler*, read *souiller* <note>corrects the spelling of "to defile"</note>; ibid. <note>Latin abbreviation for *ibidem*, meaning "in the same place"</note> *cabinetz*, read *cabinets*; p. 55. before the words, *It is in this article, etc.*, the label *Art. 7* was not included; p. 57. *Messiurs*, read *Messieurs* <note>corrects the spelling of "Gentlemen"</note>; p. 59. *conformo*, read *conforme* <note>corrects the spelling of "conforming"</note>; p. 64. *ces*, read *ce* <note>corrects the plural "these" to the singular "this"</note>; p. 74. *secte*, read *flecte* <note>Latin: corrects "sect" to "bend" or "bow"</note>; p. 91. *l’eelat*, read *l’éclat* <note>corrects the spelling of "brilliance" or "splendor"</note>; p. 92. *l’on*, read *l’ont* <note>corrects "one" to "they have"</note>; p. 97. *S. Aug. de C. D. c. 21. POST iudicium*, read *S. Aug. de C. D. l. 21. c. 13. ANTE iudicium* <note>A significant correction to a citation of Saint Augustine's *City of God*. # TO THE GENTLEMEN

## THE INHABITANTS OF SAINT JEAN
### IN THE VALLEY
### OF LUSERNA,

->Professing the Religion which they claim to be Reformed, Salvation through Jesus Christ Our Lord.<-

# GENTLEMEN.

—retics and Schismatics to the Catholic Church of all the centuries of Christianity, the sole sacred Bride of Jesus Christ, *His inheritance and His possession*.

<margin>
Matthew 13:25
</margin>
And who will wonder at this, since the enemy does not sow *the Tares in the Field of the Lord* without doing everything in his power to make them grow among the good wheat that the Master has sown there? This evil spirit has not raised up Heresiarchs <note>A leader or founder of a heretical movement.</note> within the Church without fostering these pernicious beginnings through the instruments of his obstinacy, so that he might have a great number of companions in his irreparable misery.

<margin>
2 Corinthians 11:14
</margin>
It is *the Angel of darkness transformed into an Angel of light*, who wages war against God even while speaking to Him through His word. If this liar from the beginning raises up Arians <note>Followers of Arius, a 4th-century priest who taught that Jesus Christ was a created being rather than fully God.</note>, he will place passages of Scripture in their mouths against the Mystery of the Trinity and the Divinity of the Son of God.
<margin>
John 8
</margin>

This is what he has always done from Christ and the Apostles until now, as the history of all the centuries of Christianity testifies, the event verifies, and experience provides the knowledge. More than in all preceding centuries, he has raised up a Waldo <note>Peter Waldo (c. 1140–1218), founder of the Waldensian movement or "Vaudois."</note> in these Valleys, a Luther in Germany, and a Calvin in France and Geneva, and now fosters [this] in their disciples, who—explaining the Scripture only through the commentaries of these Innovators—make you listen to it only according to the inclination of their own wills; from this has come the name of Waldensians, Lutherans, and Calvinists.

And what is most deplorable is that when someone among you prefers the oracles of Jesus Christ to the whims of these individuals, all the animosity of their partisans is unleashed upon him, as if he were an apostate from Christianity.

page 3
A 2

It is not only now that people have begun to act in this way: For experience has shown clearly enough with what fury they have always acted against those of their party who have joined the fold of the Catholic Church.

<margin>1 Peter 3:15</margin>
One only needs to look at the response given to me by the Ministers of these Valleys <note>Referring to the Waldensian (Vaudois) valleys in the Piedmont region, a stronghold of the Reformation in the Alps.</note> to see the truth of what I say: I know the falsity of your Reformation, and the truth of the Catholic Religion, from which you departed. In due time I give glory to God; I renounce the former, and embrace the latter. I provide you with the reason for it with all the *gentleness, modesty, and reverence* that Saint Peter commands <note>A reference to 1 Peter 3:15: "Always be prepared to give an answer... but do this with gentleness and respect."</note>, and yet a Company of Ministers to whom I was not even speaking rises up against me and gives me a response that bears more the marks of passion <note>In the 17th century, "passion" usually referred to uncontrolled, violent emotion or anger.</note> than the kindness of *Jesus*, whose words were nothing but sweetness and whose actions were nothing but charity.

One would need as many pages as there are lines to fully pursue their evasions; for, being well aware of this custom of the adversaries of the Catholic Church, you will have seen in the book I presented to you the rebuttal to this response, even before your gentlemen had thought to set their hands to it.

It says everything false that could be said against the Catholic Religion, and against me who embraced it, in order to make it appear in your minds as a horrific monster, and me as a marvel of iniquity. If you read it carefully, you will see such evident passion that I need no judgment other than your own to conclude that these gentlemen were then too angry to be believed in what they put forward.

The first thing they say is that I [had]...

<meta>The previous page concluded with the author describing the Protestant ministers' attempts to slander him following his conversion to Catholicism. The current page refutes the specific accusation that he tried to secretly recall his writings to hide them from scrutiny.</meta>

page 4
A

...to make it appear that I had requested the return of the copies of my writing with extraordinary diligence <note>In this context, "diligence" refers to urgent or persistent effort.</note>. Now, is the opposite of that not all too well known to you? Can one not say that a writing beginning with such a palpable falsehood—of which you are the witnesses—can only be followed and finished by propositions of the same nature? For what request did I make of you? To whom, and when? And how many times? And what were the names of those who made it on my behalf?

My diligence consisted only in giving you five months of time to make as many copies as you wished, so that, by making no difficulty in producing it when it is requested of you, a comparison can be made between it and the response of your Gentlemen Ministers. In this way, one can see the difference between their conduct and mine, and whether they were given any reason to fly into outrages as they have done.

These Gentlemen, having begun with such an evident falsehood, continue with invectives, accusing me immediately afterward of several things of which their conscience and yours sufficiently clear me. They accuse me of *ignorance* and of *malice*, without realizing the harm they do to their own reputation. For either they are more ignorant than I by placing me in the most important pulpit of these <term>Valleys</term> <note>Referring to the Waldensian (Vaudois) alpine valleys where the author previously served as a Protestant pastor.</note>, besides the other difficult tasks they gave me (which you know as well as they do); or they are guilty of malice against you by giving you an ignorant man as a <term>Preacher</term>. Did the importance of your post not deserve one of these so-learned Ministers to act toward you as people of good conscience and as men without *malice*?

I have no need of any other <term>apology</term> <note>From the Greek "apologia," meaning a formal speech or written defense of one's conduct or opinions.</note> than the one you yourselves can make to justi-

<meta>This page continues the author’s defense against the Protestant ministers who have published a "preface" attacking his character. He is responding to the charge that his six-year period of doubt and study was actually a period of "continual hypocrisy."</meta>

page 6
A

affirm with such assurance.

It was not enough for these Gentlemen to have represented me in your minds, against their own knowledge and yours, as something other than what I am; but to crown their work, they must even encroach upon the rights of God and act as **Searchers of hearts** <note>original: "Scrutateurs des cœurs." A biblical reference (e.g., Psalm 7:9) implying that only God has the power to know a person's internal thoughts and motives.</note>, saying that I remained for six years in a *continual* hypocrisy. They say this because I stated that for six years I had resolved to inform myself deeply as to which of the two Religions was the true one, and could be defended with reason. It is as if these Gentlemen were ignorant of the fact that we are not in the time of the **infused knowledge** <note>original: "Science infuse." A theological term referring to knowledge placed directly into the mind by God (as with the Apostles) rather than acquired through study.</note> of the Prophets and Apostles, but that such knowledge must be acquired with much time and labor—especially a Knowledge of this nature, which required at least six years to acquire to the degree necessary for a matter of such ultimate importance. I would like these Gentlemen to tell me: on the day they began to study Theology, did they already know what they know now, and had they already resolved to learn through those first steps?

I would be as tedious and ridiculous as they are passionate if I wished to amuse myself by refuting all those insipid insults with which they have filled their preface, without answering a single word of mine. It is enough for my consolation that the world knows the contrary, and that it is their old custom to speak in such a manner of those who leave their <term>new party</term> <note>The author uses "nouveau parti" to refer to the Protestant Reformation, contrasting its recent history with the antiquity of the Catholic Church.</note> to place themselves in the bosom of the Church of the Lord. However, while returning neither evil for evil, nor insult for insult, I wish to make you see that I am not driven by a spirit such as theirs, but that it is the spirit of truth alone, and of reason, which guides me.

<summary>The author justifies his six-year transition to Catholicism as a period of necessary study, arguing that religious certainty is earned through labor rather than instant divine inspiration. He rejects his critics' personal insults, characterizing them as a standard reaction toward those who return to the Catholic Church.</summary>

<sig>A 4</sig>

After having spewed all their venom <note>original: "fiel," literally gall. In the 17th century, "fiel" and "bile" were commonly used to describe bitter anger or spite.</note> and vented all their bile against my person, they do not fail to cast their contempt upon the manuscript I sent you, in order to dissuade you from believing it. This resentful spite appearing in their response is the inheritance of their Reformer <note>Likely referring to John Calvin, the foundational figure for French Protestants (Huguenots).</note> who *judged men as he pleased according to whether he loved or hated them*, just as his fellow Reformers reproached him.

They call the word of God and the faith of the Golden Ages <note>A reference to the early centuries of the Christian Church, which the author considers the purest era of faith.</note> that I bring to them "reheated cabbage" <note>original: "chou recuit." A common French idiom for stale, repetitive arguments that have been heard too many times before.</note> and matter chewed over a thousand times. Regarding the word of God, one should not be surprised that they "honor" it in such a way: for, believing only what accords with the logic of their own reform, one must admit that this "reheated cabbage" and this matter so often chewed over has never been digested by their stomachs—for a sick heart loathes food. <margin>Job 33:20.</margin> But there should be no doubt that this word I bring you will judge both them and you on the Last Day if you do not believe it; and for them, above all, this supposed "reheated cabbage" and "chewed matter" will be in its time—if they do not mend their ways—like the book of the Angel that will make their stomach bitter. <margin>Revelation 10:10.</margin> <note>In this biblical passage, the prophet eats a scroll that tastes sweet as honey in the mouth but turns bitter in the stomach.</note>

They say that this is a piece that does not deserve a response, against which one should only oppose a *Cyrus du Moulin* <note>Cyrus de Moulin was a prominent 17th-century Protestant minister and writer of "controversy" books.</note> or other small books of religious debate. Certainly, Gentlemen, if you wait to value and believe the Catholic truths until your Ministers praise them to you, you will perish in your errors: For even if they came, not

<meta>This page continues the author’s defense against the Protestant ministers (the "Adversaries"). Having previously addressed their mockery of his "reheated" arguments, he now contrasts their intellectual pride with the humble truth of the Catholic Church, quoting St. Augustine to illustrate the stubbornness of those who have broken away from the fold.</meta>

<page-num>8</page-num>

not of me, but of that which is most sacred under Heaven, they will always despise these things if they do not accord with their own thoughts—just as St. Augustine <note>A foundational 4th-century theologian and Bishop of Hippo, often cited by both Catholics and Protestants to claim legitimacy.</note> relates of those separated from the Church in his time: that only what they themselves desired *was Holy*.

Knowing, then, their habitual contempt for everything that contradicts them, however true it may be, it is not to them, but to you that I have addressed my writing. For, being as ignorant as they claim I am, how would I have dared to undertake the instruction of such "subtle Doctors," <note>A sarcastic reference to the Protestant ministers, implying they are overly intellectual and prideful.</note> as they seem to imagine themselves to be? <margin>1 Peter 5:5.</margin> *It is to the humble that God gives grace*, says St. Peter; likewise, it is to you that I communicate the light God has given me. It is for you to refuse to be blinded by their passion, and instead to believe the truth when it is addressed to you by the sole motive of charity, so that the truth might set <margin>John 8:32.</margin> your souls free from the slavery in which the hatred of the Adversaries of the Church keeps you plunged.

<margin>Institutes 4. 9. 8. <note>A reference to John Calvin’s primary work, "Institutes of the Christian Religion." The author is likely pointing to a specific passage where Calvin discusses the authority of councils or the Church.</note></margin>
In this little manuscript that I have sent you, there is nothing that I myself value, except for the word of God and the shining Faith of the Golden Ages <note>The "Golden Ages" refers to the era of the Early Church Fathers (the first few centuries AD), which the author views as the standard of pure Christian doctrine.</note> with which I have bordered the margins. I myself despise what is mine; but I venerate there, as I must, the testimony of the Spirit of God and the language of His Church.

These Gentlemen <note>The Protestant ministers.</note> were too credulous to imagine that this was all I intended to say to you, or that it was the extent of my proof that I neither should nor could speak against the Catholic Church nor its doctrines. For that [manuscript] being only a short summary of a printed treatise that I have offered you—so that whoever wishes may profit from it—you will grant me that it...

<meta>This page continues the author's address to those who have left the Catholic Church for Protestantism. it was not yet time to take all this trouble.

<margin>Matthew 5.</margin>
Let them say as much as they please that the motives I have proposed to you are but a tissue of errors; Heaven will pass away sooner than they shall annihilate one iota <note>original: "iota" — A reference to Matthew 5:18: "For truly I tell you, until heaven and earth disappear, not the smallest letter [iota], not the least stroke of a pen, will by any means disappear from the Law."</note> of the Word of God that I bring to it. And yet the day will approach on which they will have to give an account for your souls, and feel the weight of the anathemas <term>Anathema: A formal curse or excommunication by a religious authority, signifying a person is cut off from the community of believers.</term> under which they cause your souls to perish through their discourses, and which will not fail to be struck against them by God, just as they are by the Church according to the promise that Jesus Christ made to her:
<margin>Matthew 16:19</margin>
->*Whatever you bind on Earth shall be bound in Heaven.*<-

May the Lord give them, before that day, stinging remorse and the just reproaches of their conscience for the wrong they do you, so that all together, having but one Father <note>God the Father.</note> and one Mother <note>Commonly referring to the Church as "Holy Mother Church."</note> on earth, we may one day receive the same inheritance in Heaven.

Meanwhile, hurling back at them all their contempt, and denying everything they oppose to me in favor of their novelty <note>In 17th-century religious debate, "novelty" was a common insult used by Catholics against Protestants, implying their faith was a recent invention rather than the original Christian faith.</note>, I want to show you that the so-called Reformation they preach to you was never, as they say, *the Religion of your Fathers*; it was never entirely pure, entirely holy, nor drawn from the living springs of the Word of God, and it was never true that it had none but Jesus Christ as its Author.

And on the contrary, I want to show you that all these praises belong only to the Catholic Church, from which you and your fathers have recently separated yourselves, and to maintain to you that this Catholic Religion...

--- Page 30 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's appeal to his Protestant audience, specifically responding to the arguments of their ministers. These Gentlemen seem to take pleasure in making themselves *ridiculous* by the objections they raise to me regarding some difference of observations in the Church of the first Christians converted from Paganism and Judaism: For firstly, there is a great difference between a Church that is newly establishing itself and one that has been established for a long time. In the second place, did these first Christians hold separate assemblies and communion? And did they oppose temple against temple, as do today the Lutherans and Calvinists in Germany and elsewhere, and the Episcopalians <note>Members of the Church of England/Anglicans who retained a hierarchy of bishops.</note> and Presbyterians in England?

# Article III.

Gentlemen, your Ministers <term>Ministers: The author uses this term to refer to Protestant pastors or theologians.</term> continue their efforts to shake the Columns <note>The author uses "Columns" as a metaphor for the foundational arguments or "pillars" of his faith as presented in his earlier summary.</note> that I showed you in my summary. They address this one with all their power, and spare no rhetorical engines to shake it, strike it down, and render it invisible—all to keep their own church hidden in invisibility, and to exempt themselves from the labor of answering the request made to them continually: to show their Church before the time of Waldo, Luther, and Calvin. <note>Peter Waldo (12th century), Martin Luther, and John Calvin (16th century) were key figures in movements that preceded or formed the Protestant Reformation. The author is challenging the idea that a Protestant-style church existed visibly before these men.</note>

Instead of responding to the proofs of this article, and then opposing it with the passages they pile up against its truth—as the proper order of every treatise requires—they begin with this volley of poorly applied passages. Seeing them in such disorder at the start of their attack gives me assurance of their coming defeat. I am going to follow them there to cause this effort to evaporate more quickly, and then return to see this Column in its proper state.

<margin>Revelation 13</margin>
Citing what is said, that *all the inhabitants of the earth will worship the Beast*, they ask so as to quickly have the answer: *where then will the visible Church be?*

<margin>Verse 8</margin>
I say that she will be among the number of those whose names are written in the book of the Lamb, as is inferred from what the same Verse says: that *those whose names are not written in the book of life of the Lamb will worship the Beast.*

Ask these Gentlemen for a passage of Scripture that proves that those who will not worship the Beast will be invisible to the world; and I will prove to them that just as they were visible in Babylon, and effectively seen and discerned from others,

the heresies of the heretics—something we do not deny and which is not at all the question at hand, nor something I spoke to them about. They waste time shadow-boxing <note>original: "escrimer contre l'air", literally "fencing against the air," a metaphor for fighting an imaginary opponent</note> rather than addressing the points, simply to pad their written submissions <note>original: "Cahiers", referring to the formal theological papers or notebooks produced by his opponents</note> and distract you with words.

They were so hostile toward my writing that instead of following it step-by-step through its entire progression—matching preface for preface and article for article—they instead filled their preface with insults. They scatter their responses to my reasons here and there to dilute their force, which they dismissively label as *pomp*. I intend to rebut their objections to my proofs and follow them visibly until they retreat back into the invisibility that they find so appealing.

And first, I ask you to notice that when the passages of Revelation <note>original: "Apocalipse"</note> which they cited against me speak of a *Town* or *City*, they are quite willing for it to be understood as *the Church*. However, when I <margin>Matthew 5:14</margin> show them the *City situated on a mountain*, they insist that this is only said of the *Apostles*, because <margin>Matthew 5</margin> he had previously told them that they were the *Light of the World*.

Now, it is easy to convict them by their own logic. For if it is not only the Apostles who are the *light of the World* and who must let the light of their good works shine before men, but rather all good Christians—as <margin>Ephesians 5:8</margin> Saint Paul teaches the Ephesians: *you are light, walk therefore as children of light*—then it is also not only of the Apostles that Jesus Christ says they are the *city built on a Mountain*. Instead, he speaks of the whole Christian Church, of which the Apostles were the illustrious torches through the truth of their Doctrine and the holiness of their lives.

# Article IV.

There is not one of those who have risen up against the Church of God during the past 16 centuries who did not first undertake to dethrone its Sovereign <term>Pontiff</term> <note>The Pope, the Bishop of Rome</note>; <term>Luther</term> <note>Martin Luther (1483–1546), the central figure of the Protestant Reformation</note> began there, and all his followers have continued to assail him until now, believing that if they brought this Head to the ground, they would easily overcome the whole Body. <margin>John 10.</margin> They know that *if the Shepherd is struck, the sheep will be scattered* <note>An allusion to Zechariah 13:7, quoted in the Gospels regarding the arrest of Jesus</note>. The Gentlemen Ministers of these Valleys <note>The pastors of the Waldensian (Vaudois) communities in the Alpine valleys of Piedmont</note> are no more favorable to him than the others, and it is especially on this point that their animosity is displayed, as you can see in what they have opposed to this Article, where I had said that the **Christian Church** must have a **visible and ministerial Head** who leads and governs it under **Christ**, and in his name, just as under the Old Testament he governed the Jewish Church through the **Sovereign Priests**, **Aaron** and his successors, and under the Gospel he had given to **Saint Peter**, the *first* of the **Apostles**, the **Keys of this Celestial Kingdom**.

<margin>Matthew 10</margin>

Who is the *Head* of a Body if not he who presides? Who is the *Head* of a house and of a city if not he who has the conduct and the government of it, signified by the **Keys**? <meta>This page continues the author's argument that the Christian Church requires a central authority. ...ship which must exist in the New Testament among the **Leaders of the Christian Church**. Calvin says that <margin>a</margin> *the Jewish people had to have a Sovereign Prince to hold them in unity, so that they would not be distracted by various religions*. Does the **Christian Church** not have need of this unity? Does **Saint Paul** not call it a **Body**? And did our Lord not say <margin>Song of Solomon 6:9</margin> through **Solomon** that *the dove is one?* <note>In traditional Catholic commentary, the "dove" in the Song of Solomon is interpreted as a symbol for the singular, unified Church.</note> Why then, to hold it in this *Unity*, should it not be given a **Leading Head** whose authority over all more easily maintains them all in *Unity* of spirit, of faith, and of obedience? Certainly, if there is more difficulty in maintaining *Unity* among many than among few, the **Christian Church** has much more need of a **visible and ministerial Head** than the **Jewish Church**, because the former is composed of many nations, while the latter was composed of only one.

Not one of all the **Catholic Doctors** <note>The "Doctors" refers to the authoritative theologians and scholars of the Catholic Church.</note> who have used this reasoning to prove a *Head* in the Church has become a Jew; I assure **Monsieur Gros**, the author of the response, that he need not fear—as he does—that I shall become one any more than they, simply for having said that it is right to conform to the order of God. The itch he had to slander and mock reason has led him, as well as his colleagues, to say that the claim I make regarding the **Keys** given to St. Peter by Jesus Christ is ridiculous and absurd. This is despite the fact that it is clear that by these Keys our Lord intended to show that he was conferring upon St. Peter the government of the entire Church, which is the house and family of the living God, his Spiritual and Heavenly Kingdom. This is much like *the key of the house of David* that God placed upon the shoulder of Eliakim, which was—according to the admission of Diodati <note>Giovanni Diodati (1576–1649) was a Swiss-born Italian Protestant theologian who translated the Bible into Italian; even he, a Protestant, acknowledged this symbol of authority.</note>—*a sign of his general superintendence over his Palace*, <margin>Is. 22:22</margin> which is expressed by the addition: *he shall open and none shall shut, he shall shut and none shall open.*

And these gentlemen are more than ridiculous in the reason they offer: namely, that our Lord does not speak there of a **Head**. It is as if having the government of this entire family and Kingdom were not the same thing as being its Head, especially since by this he conferred upon him an unreserved authority: *whatsoever you bind on earth shall be bound in Heaven.* *He receives these Keys*, says the great Pope Gregory, whom Calvin calls a *Holy Man*, *the power of binding and loosing is given to him; the care and the PRINCIPALITY of the whole Church is committed to him.* <note>The author is using the Protestant reformer John Calvin’s own high opinion of Pope Gregory the Great to validate Gregory's defense of Papal authority.</note> And here is a Holy Pope who answers the question of your ministers: *How can one draw from this a consequence for the primacy of St. Peter?*

Just as nurses have the word "Wolf" on their lips for the small children they wish to intimidate, so Calvin and the Ministers of *his Reformation* habitually have the word *Antichrist* on their lips to keep their people away from the Pope.

And because this is the "big word" these Gentlemen use to prevent their *poor listeners* from submitting to the *authority of his Keys* <note>A reference to the "Keys of the Kingdom of Heaven" promised to St. Peter in the Gospel of Matthew, symbolizing the Pope's spiritual authority.</note>, I have inserted this point among the others. I do so not to *set traps* for anyone—having always been more ready to pull out those who are already in them, whenever I have been able, than to set or throw anyone into one—but rather to remove from your mind the opinion that these Gentlemen plant there like a *scarecrow in a garden* to frighten away the birds.

It is a foolishness <note>The original French "niaiserie" suggests a trivial or nonsensical absurdity.</note> to say, as they do, that I drafted this Article in a way that reeks of silliness, *purposely to set traps for them, and to have grounds to accuse them before the higher powers* should they happen to say something against this *Pontiff*. For, in the first place, I was not speaking to them; and since I was not asking them whether they should or should not speak, it is a very great foolishness to say that I did this to have cause to accuse them. This is much like when some of them spread rumors throughout all these Valleys that for six years I have been an *accuser, traitor, and secret informant* <term>fiscal secret<note>A "procureur fiscal" was a legal official; here, the author is being accused of being a secret agent who reports legal or religious infractions to the government.</note></term> to report to the *Adversaries of their Religion* everything that was done in the Valleys, and to incite against them what they claim was done against their welfare,

<meta>The previous page concluded with the author defending himself against the charge of drafting his article on the Antichrist with malicious intent to entrap his opponents. ...and that I drew 200 livres a year in pension for these good services; a rumor spread intentionally to blacken my name everywhere, and to make me an object of horror, especially to you.

Their *foolishness* <note>The original French "niaiserie" carries a sense of both simplicity and absurdity.</note> is no less revealed in the manifest contradiction they fall into when saying that this Article *smacks of foolishness*, expressly intended *to set traps for them*. For being foolish and setting *traps* are things that do not belong to the same person, as it is not the nature of a fool to *set traps* [such people know neither how to *set* them, nor how to protect themselves from those *set* for them], but rather that of the cunning and malicious. Now let us see with what skill they get themselves out of it.

They wish to say nothing regarding the *Antichrist* except what *Holy Scripture* declares about him. And yet, all of a sudden, they depart from it, putting forward a distinction of their own invention: that is, they say the word *Antichrist* signifies two things: namely, first *against Christ*, and second *in the stead and place of Christ*.

In the first sense, they place all his enemies <margin>[1 John 2 and 4]</margin> such as the Jews, Turks <note>In the 17th century, "Turks" was commonly used as a general term for Muslims.</note>, Pagans, Socinians <note>A group of radical reformers who denied the Trinity and the full divinity of Christ.</note>, and all those who deny the Divinity of the Son of God.

In the second sense, they station the one who is represented by his signs in the second epistle of St. Paul to the Thes- <margin>2 Thess. 2</margin> salonians; *Who will be seated in the Temple of God as God, conducting himself as if he were God*, and then they leave me to guess who that might be, alleging various things done toward the Pope of Rome, and things said about him by the Doctors of the Roman Church, to make it believed that he is the one St. Paul depicts in this passage by these signs and liveries <note>The "livery" refers to the distinctive marks or identifying clothing of a high office.</note>, which they do solely out of their passion, and not out of reason.

--- Page 65 ---
<meta>Continuity from page 44: The author continues his rebuttal of the Protestant definition of the Antichrist. Having established that his opponents define the term in two ways—"against Christ" and "in the place of Christ"—he now analyzes why these definitions fail to prove that the Pope is the Antichrist.</meta>

I pray you to note first that in these words reported by these Gentlemen <note>The author refers to the Protestant ministers or theologians he is debating.</note>, the Apostle does not speak of Christ, nor of anyone who is against Christ, nor in his place, so as to be this Antichrist: but he speaks of God, and of him who shall carry himself as God: in which case he should rather be called an *Antigod* <note>original: "Antidieu"</note> than an *Antichrist*.

In the second place: regarding the first sense mentioned above, as these Gentlemen do not include the Pope of Rome, but rather those who deny the Divinity of the Son of God—such as Jews, Turks <note>In this period, "Turks" was a common synecdoche for all Muslims.</note>, Pagans, Socinians <note>A sect that denied the Trinity and the pre-existence of Christ.</note>, and other such people—it follows that the Pope is not the Antichrist in that sense, by their own confession.

Regarding the second sense, in which they want the word *Antichrist* to be taken, I respond that someone is in the stead and in the place of another either by usurpation and to his exclusion—as a Tyrant puts himself in the place of a legitimate King to reign there to the exclusion of the one he excludes, and in his own name, as Cromwell <note>Oliver Cromwell (1599–1658), who ruled England as Lord Protector.</note> did in England to the exclusion of Charles I and of Charles II, his legitimate heir now reigning <note>This reference suggests the text was written after the Stuart Restoration in 1660.</note>—or by the will, consent, and order of him who is the Master and legitimate possessor, to hold his place and authority, and to command there only in his name, as a Viceroy <term>Viceroy: A governor ruling a representative territory on behalf of a monarch.</term> in the place and in the absence of the King.

He who shall be seated in the *Temple of God* as God, carrying himself as God, will be in that place in the first sense I have just laid out; that is to say, as a Tyrant and Usurper, to be recognized, obeyed, and honored to the exclusion of the true God whom he will deny—namely, the Father and the Son—boasting of being himself this God and this Christ, just as Jesus said the false Prophets, his precursors, will say: *the Christ is here, he is there*, and the Apostle <margin>Matt. 24</margin>.

<summary>The author argues that the biblical description of a figure exalting himself in the Temple refers to an "Antigod" or usurper rather than the Pope. He uses the historical example of Cromwell's usurpation of the English throne to distinguish between a legitimate representative (like a Viceroy) and an illegitimate tyrant.</summary>

<meta>Continuity from page 45: The author is concluding his argument that being "in the place of" someone is not the same as being "against" them by usurpation. He now turns to specific scriptural definitions of the Antichrist to show the Pope does not fit these descriptions.</meta>

<margin>1 John 2</margin>
St. John teaches that the Antichrist will deny the Father and the Son, and will deny that Christ has come in the flesh. That person is the one who will come in his own name, and of whom Jesus said to the Jews, <margin>Matthew 24:5</margin> *I have come in my Father's name, and you have not received me; another will come in his own name, and you will receive him.* <note>While the margin cites Matthew 24:5, the phrasing "I have come in my Father's name..." corresponds to John 5:43.</note> And that is the Antichrist who is to come, of whom Jesus Christ, St. Paul, and St. John speak, who, by denying the Father and the Son, will present himself as God, and will want to be recognized as the Christ who was to come.

But the Pope of Rome has never denied the Father and the Son, nor that Christ has come in the flesh; rather, he adores Him, and applies all care and diligence to make Him adored throughout the whole earth. Therefore, he is not that Antichrist.

It is quite another thing to be in the place of Christ by his order and authority, under him, and in his name. For just as a Viceroy is not an "Antiking" <note>original: "Antiroi"</note>, so too the Pope of Rome is not an Antichrist. Instead, he stands in the place of Christ—who is absent from us in his flesh—to whom he refers his authority and government. He recognizes himself not only as a *Servant*, but as the *servant of his Servants* <note>original: "seruiteur de fes Seruiteurs"; this translates the traditional papal title *Servus servorum Dei*.</note>, just as Popes Damasus and St. Gregory the Great did during the first six centuries of the purity of the Christian Church; and in their imitation, the Popes who have followed them until now do the same.

I add to this that the Antichrist of whom St. Paul speaks must perform lying miracles, even *making fire fall from Heaven before men*; but as the Popes of Rome have never done this, they are not that Antichrist.

In a word, the Antichrist of whom St. Paul speaks will be seated in the Temple of God—that is to say, his Church—as everyone admits. But the Gentlemen Ministers <note>The author refers to Protestant pastors, likely in the Alpine regions where the Waldensian "Valleys" are located.</note> of these Valleys say at the beginning of this article that *where the Antichrist is, there is the Synagogue of Satan*, and they want me to conclude, by the marks and books they have brought me, that the Pope of Rome is the Antichrist, and consequently his Church the Synagogue of Satan. And yet, it follows that by being seated there, he is not that Antichrist of whom the Apostle says here that he will be seated in the *Temple of God*; for the Temple, being nothing other than *his Church*, cannot be the *Synagogue of the Devil*. And that is how the calumny falls into contradiction and shows its falsehood; and how much the Holy Pontiff is obliged to them for justifying him themselves against that of which they accuse him.

# Article VI.

Gentlemen, your Ministers admit that they have separated themselves from the Roman Church, and that they were right to do so; the Roman Church, on the contrary, maintains that they were wrong to separate from its Communion. You will admit to me, Gentlemen, that this is the principal point upon which all others depend; for if you were right to separate from the Roman Church, as long as that reason persists, you do well to continue in this separation. But if you were wrong to do so, and the reason you had then was void, and remains so now, you are obliged to repair this wrong by returning to the Chur-

And just as the Roman Catholic Church has always flourished among these heretics, like the *Lily* among the Thorns, she still flourishes now in the midst of all her enemies; this *City* remains well-united together notwithstanding the breaches made by those besieging it. This *Pillar and Ground of Truth* does not fall to the ground, because Jesus Christ, who has sustained her for nearly 17 centuries, will sustain her still against all her adversaries.

I had called the peoples who followed after the *Barbes* <term>Barbes: From the regional word for "uncle," this was the title given to the pastors of the Waldensians (Vaudois), a group the author associates with the Protestant movement.</term> by the name of their followers, not wishing to call them by that of *Sect*, for fear of offending them—as they are accustomed to taking offense when they are called by that name. But since these gentlemen call themselves the *Sect* of these figures, they will no longer be offended if they are called such, because in doing so one only uses their own terms. And it is here that I wish to show the difference between my departure from their midst and theirs from the bosom of the Catholic Church.

Ils <note>original: "Ils" - This is the catchword, indicating the first word of the next page.</note>

They call the Roman Church by the name *Church*, and their own party by the name *Sect*. There is, therefore, this difference: when they left the Roman Church, they left the *Church*; and when I left their communion, I left a *Sect* by their own admission—not to create a separate Sect and divide the unity of the Church as they have done, but to return to the Church which I had left following my predecessors <note>The author suggests he was born into Protestantism because of his ancestors, but his "return" corrects that ancestral error.</note>. Thus, whatever they may know how to say, their departure was unjust and reckless, and mine was just and necessary. Consequently, the displeasure the Roman Church felt at their departure was for good reason, but not so the displeasure they felt at mine, since they say at the beginning of their response that I left their midst because I was not one of them <note>A reference to 1 John 2:19: "They went out from us, but they were not of us."</note>.

They say that **Waldo** <note>Peter Waldo (c. 1140 – c. 1205), the founder of the Waldensians, whom the author views as a precursor to the Reformation.</note> and his **Barbes**, **Luther**, **Calvin**, and their **Sect** were forced to leave the **Roman Church** by Violence, persecutions, imprisonments, crusades, tortures, executions, proscriptions, banishments, and the confiscation of their property.

I answer that this great heap of words is but a long web of falsehoods, for the **Roman Church** never forced them to leave her bosom by any of those means. And if she used any force against them, it was not to make them leave, as they say, but to punish the audacity of their departure after it had occurred without reason. And after having employed all the gentleness imaginable, if she finally made use of severity, it was only to make them return to the **Church** from which they had departed, imitating in this the Christians of the pure and holy primitive Church toward those who, in their time, also formed separate **Sects** and divided the union of the **Church**.

Reason itself dictates it so: for if the disturbers of the civil peace of a State deserve punishment, why not the disturbers of the peace of all **Christendom** spread throughout the world? And above all, the peace of Conscience and the Soul? What do they themselves not do in the places where they are Masters against those who hold opposing views? And especially against **Catholics**? This is too well known to the whole world to recount it here, so true is what the ancient **Holy Doctors** <note>The "Church Fathers," early influential Christian theologians.</note> say: that those separated from the **Church** wish indeed to judge everyone, but wish to be judged by no one.

These Gentlemen, seeing themselves alone in upholding the **calling** <note>A "calling" or "vocation" refers to the divine authority or legitimate right to serve as a minister and lead a church.</note> of their Reformers in these Valleys, and without hope of succeeding with honor, are very glad to make an alliance with those from outside who have received the reform of **Luther**, **Calvin**, and others; and in imitation of their predecessors from the time of those extraordinary personages, they leave behind their **Waldensian Reform**, and take up that of **Calvin** and **Oecolampadius** <note>Johannes Oecolampadius (1482–1531) was a leader of the Reformation in Basel, Switzerland.</note> in France, Geneva, and Switzerland; for as for that of England and Germany, they do not adopt it, keeping to those closest to them so as to have help more readily in case of need.

They are not any stronger for this, for it was a long time ago that the Roman Church showed them that the calling of **Luther** and **Calvin** was no more legitimate than that of **Waldo** and his Waldensian ***Barbes*** <note>original: "Barbes"; the traditional title for Waldensian pastors, from the regional word for "uncle."</note>. And all together, they have no other calling than that of their own private spirit, as their contradictions and the opposing parties they have formed clearly show, excommunicating one another as heretics and blasphemers, just as I have proven above by their own [testimony].

Having previously argued that the Waldensians abandoned their own traditions for Calvinist ones, the author now addresses the logical arguments used by Protestants to justify their "calling" to preach without traditional Catholic ordination.</meta>

<page-num>59</page-num>

They commit their usual error in reasoning, which is called **begging the question** <term>original: *petition de principe*; a logical fallacy where the premise of an argument assumes the truth of the conclusion instead of proving it</term>, by claiming that it was necessary at that time to convince the idolaters, to rescue men from the darkness of ignorance, and to confound error and lies through the Word of God. To this end, they argue that their **Reformers** received an **inner and immediate calling** <note>original: *vocation interieure*; the Protestant claim that God directly appointed their leaders to ministry, bypassing the need for the traditional succession of Catholic bishops.</note> from God, and that they could produce no finer "patent" <note>A legal document or certificate granting a right or title.</note> to justify their mission than the "pure doctrine" they preached, which they claim is in total conformity with the Word of God.

These are nothing but beautiful "castles in the air"—making assertions while proving nothing, building without a foundation, and reasoning without logic. They presuppose as true and certain something that never was, and never will be: that the **Roman Church** was mired in idolatry, the darkness of ignorance, and in error and lies, and that they alone have preached the pure Word of God.

There are already so many books that prove their claims are false in all these matters that no more are needed for that purpose. And if such reasoning were as valid as it is actually ridiculous, there is no Christian today who could not, on such a foundation, build their own separate sect and shatter the unity of the Church, having no other oracle or miracle to rely on than their own private claims and the dictates of their own caprice.

<margin>Acts 11:19–20</margin>
They say that the inhabitants of **Jerusalem** who were scattered here and there in **Judea** and **Samaria** announced the Word of God, and that **Philip** preached **Christ** with signs and miracles. However, those people announced the message to the **Jews** only, as is seen in Chapter 11 of the **Acts of the Apostles**, and also to the **Greeks**.

If **Luther** and **Calvin** had gone throughout the world to convert the heathens and announce the **Gospel**...

<margin>2 Thessalonians 2:15</margin> which recommends holding fast to *the Traditions given by word, as well as those given by Epistle* <note>original: "par parole, comme celles qui sont données par Epitre." Judge for yourselves, Gentlemen, that in speaking this way, far from contradicting myself, I prove all the better that Holy Scripture is a certain rule of what I must believe, because it commands me to believe the Traditions given by word.

It is they, on the contrary, who fall into manifest contradiction: on one hand saying that this written word is a certain rule of what we must believe, and on the other hand refusing to receive these Traditions given by word which are recommended by St. Paul.

There is a great difference between saying that the written word is the certain rule of what we must believe and do, and saying that it is *alone*, and without Traditions, the perfect rule of *everything* we must believe *and do*. I subscribe to the first proposition as a Catholic: but I deny the second as being contrary to Scripture and to the Doctrine of the Orthodox Church <note>In the 17th century, Catholic writers frequently used the term "Orthodox" (meaning "right-believing") to refer to the Roman Catholic Church in opposition to what they viewed as the "heterodox" views of the Reformers.</note>.

...any more than they could prove to me that man lives by bread alone, simply because it is true that he lives by bread <gloss>a</gloss>, *the affirmation of one thing not being the negation of the other*. <note>The author argues that saying "Bread sustains life" doesn't mean "Only bread sustains life." Likewise, saying "Scripture is the word of God" doesn't mean "Only Scripture is the word of God."</note>

Scripture fulfills everything that the aforementioned passages say; everything it contains is true and capable of establishing an Article of Faith <term>Article of Faith: A fundamental doctrine of the Church considered necessary for salvation.</term> for eternal life. However, it does not contain all the truths that one must believe for salvation, and in this lies its perfection: that what it does not contain, it shows us where it *is* contained—namely, in the Traditions handed down by word of mouth, which for this purpose it commands us to *hold fast*. In this, as in what it contains, it makes a person *wise unto salvation, and perfect in every good work*.

<margin>Galatians 1:8</margin>
I know that St. Paul says: *Though we ourselves, or an Angel from Heaven, should preach a gospel to you beyond what we have preached to you, let him be accursed*; but I reply—using the marginal note from the Geneva Bible <note>The Geneva Bible was a prominent Protestant translation; the author is cleverly using his opponents' own resources to prove his point.</note>—that this word *beyond* means *otherwise* than what we have preached to you. This does nothing to oppose the Traditions in question. Whether St. Paul meant *otherwise* or *against* by this *beyond*, there is reason to believe it, for this very Apostle himself later taught many other things than what he had preached at that time. And did not St. John the Divine <note>original: "S. Iean le Theologien"</note>, who lived about thirty years after St. Paul, preach and write the Revelations of the Apocalypse?

<margin>Galatians 1:8. Beyond what we preached. <note>original Greek: "Par' à euenguelis ámetha"</note></margin>
<margin>Romans 16:17. Contrary to the teaching. <note>original Greek: "Parà tèn didachèn"</note></margin>
It is in this same sense that the Apostle used this word in the 16th chapter of Romans: *I urge you, brothers, to watch out for those who cause divisions and offenses beyond* (that is to say, against) *the Doctrine you have learned*. The words of St. Chryso— <note>The text cuts off, likely referring to St. John Chrysostom, a famous early Christian preacher and Church Father.</note>

<meta>This page continues the author's argument against Protestant interpretations of St. Paul's Epistle to the Galatians. He specifically addresses the views of Pierre Gilles, a prominent Protestant minister, regarding the authority of St. John Chrysostom and the nature of Apostolic Tradition.</meta>

[Chryso]stome, the original text of which these Gentlemen should have cited in the margin for a more solid understanding of what he says, does nothing for their cause.

This excellent servant of God—as the late <gloss>a</gloss> Pierre Gilles, Minister at La Tour <note>Pierre Gilles (1571–1646) was a Protestant pastor and historian of the Waldensian Church. "La Tour" refers to Torre Pellice, a center of the Reformation in the Piedmontese valleys.</note>, calls him—distinguishes in this passage between things that are contrary to the Gospel and overturn *everything*, and those that merely shake something of the Gospel, however small it may be. If he says that St. Paul does not speak of those who announce the former, he nonetheless admits that he speaks of those who preach the latter. How does that count against the Traditions in question, which must not only avoid being contrary to the Gospel so as to *overturn* it, but also must not be foreign to it so as not to *shake* it? What does that do against the Roman Church and the Traditions she retains by the order of the same St. Paul?

Do you believe, Gentlemen, that these Traditions given by word of mouth shook in the slightest those he had given by Letter? And that his unwritten word was distant from his written word? No; rather, one must indeed believe that they agreed well together, as they were inspired by the same Spirit of truth who guided this sacred chosen vessel <note>original: "sacré Vaisseau d’élite"—a traditional title for St. Paul, based on Acts 9:15, where he is called a "chosen vessel" to carry God's name.</note>.

<margin>Galatians 1:8</margin>
Finally, the question being: have the Apostles written down everything they evangelized? Instead of proving what is contested, they try to prove what is not in question; for St. Paul does not say *besides what we have written*, but *besides what we* <note>The sentence ends abruptly here, to be continued on the next page.</note>

<meta>This page continues the author's argument from page 65 regarding the authority of Church Tradition. to free themselves from the force of the passage of St. Paul in his second [Epistle] to the Thessalonians: *Hold the Traditions which you have received from me, whether by word or by Epistle.* This authority presses them so closely that they do not know how to escape.

They say that these Traditions are the teachings he gave to the faithful of Thessalonica, either by preaching aloud or by putting them in writing in his Epistles; to this I agree.

They say that in the other Epistles and other books of Scripture the fullness of the Doctrine that St. Paul taught *verbally* is contained; this I deny.

They demand that I show them that the Traditions of the Roman Church are the same as those St. Paul commands us to *hold*; I am not obliged to do so. I need only prove to them that there are Traditions given by word besides those given by *Epistle*, as St. Paul testifies in formal terms.

One must be quite skeptical not to base one's faith on such express testimonies. *It is clear*, (says on this passage the excellent Servant of God St. John Chrysostom <gloss>a</gloss> <note>St. John Chrysostom (c. 347–407 AD) was an important Early Church Father and Archbishop of Constantinople, known for his eloquent preaching.</note> whom they inappropriately opposed to me above) *that the Apostles did not hand down everything by Epistle: but several things without writing. Now, both these and those are worthy of the same faith.*

# Article IX.

I had said in this Article that I could not preach to you that the Roman Church was *idolatrous*, because I saw in her neither *idol* nor *worship of an idol*, <term>latria<note>The author uses the word "latrie," referring to "latria"—a theological term for the supreme worship that is due to God alone. Idolatry, therefore, is giving this specific form of worship to an object.</note></term> from which the word *idolatry* comes.

These Gentlemen make every effort to make you believe the contrary, and by believing it, to make you hold this Church in horror, as guilty of the greatest crime there is before the Divine Majesty; but it has been a long time since this Church proved that their accusation is false, and therefore they are guilty of all the imaginable wrong of attributing such an enormity to her. I will not dwell much on refuting these calumnies so as not to repeat here everything I said in the Italian Book that I presented to you.

<margin>Chap. 9.</margin>
<margin>1 Cor. 8:4</margin>
You are not unaware that St. Paul says that *an idol is nothing in the world*, because it represents things that never were [like the idols of the false Gods of the Pagans] and represents things that exist: but are not what they are imagined to be, such as the Sun, the Moon, and other things that the Infidels imagined to be Gods, and to which
<margin>Rom. 1:25</margin>
they rendered Divine honor, *worshiping the Creature instead of the Creator*: but that the Roman Church has never, thank God, believed or done anything similar, is a thing too clear to all to need proof.

To this allegation, however, I respond first: that Canon 36 of the said Council <note>The Council of Elvira (c. 306 AD) is famous for its 36th Canon, which stated that "pictures ought not to be in churches." And who will be surprised if these Ministers of these Valleys—who conceive the mysteries of the Kingdom of Heaven only through the spirit of those men, and receive them only from their pens—reject everything most sacred in the Church whenever it testifies against them?

This foundation being laid, it is easy to demolish everything that your Gentlemen <note>A common way for Catholic authors of this period to refer to Protestant ministers or "Ministers of the Word."</note> oppose to it;
<margin>1 Samuel 7:3</margin>They first oppose what Samuel said to the whole house of Israel: *prepare your heart for the Eternal and serve Him alone.*

I beg you to notice how aptly they speak: the subject is the invocation of the Saints, and they bring me a passage regarding the <term>Worship</term> <note>original: "Culte." In this context, it refers to the highest form of religious adoration (latria) due to God alone.</note> and service of God, as if the invocation of the Saints and the service of God were not two very different things—though not opposed to one another—thus defending themselves where they are not being attacked (though against all the rules of good <term>disputation</term> <note>A formal academic or theological debate.</note>) so that they always have something with which to distract you.

I add to this that the passage in question concerns the service that must be rendered to God to the exclusion of the false gods of the Gentiles, as the words immediately preceding clearly show: *If you return to the Eternal with all your heart, put away the foreign gods from among you, and the Ashtaroth.* <note>Ashtaroth refers to the idols of a Canaanite goddess; the author argues the Bible here condemns pagan idolatry, not the honor given to Christian saints.</note> Where you see that Samuel forbids the house of Israel to serve any other God but the Eternal. What does that do, I ask you, to prove that one must not pray to the Saints to pray to God for us? Who ever told them that by doing this, one considers them as Gods, and renders to them the service [due to God alone?]

One must be very ignorant not to know, or very malicious to hide, that we serve God as God, the Saints as Saints, and all men according to the condition they hold, and according to the relationship we have with them. I serve God as God and Sovereign Lord, Master, and Author of all things. I serve the Saints as Servants of God, righteous in faith, and pleasing to the God they adore. I serve men with the service I owe them; therefore, am I an <term>idolater</term> <note>One who worships a created thing as if it were God.</note> who renders to the <term>Creature</term> <note>original: "Creature." In theological terms, any created being (including humans and angels) as opposed to the Creator.</note> the service I owe only to the Creator? Judge this logic for yourselves; if it is sound, why do they expect to be served by their own servants?

Continuing to run off the path, the more they run, the further they wander from it. They call the distinction between the worship of <term>*latria*</term> <note>From the Greek 'latreia'; the supreme worship and adoration due to God alone.</note> and <term>*dulia*</term> <note>From the Greek 'douleia'; the veneration or honor given to saints and angels as servants of God.</note> a fantasy; the former being due to God alone, and the latter fitting for Angels and Saints—a distinction which the <margin>a</margin> Doctors of the Church, even those they call *Holy* and *Angelic* <note>A reference to St. Thomas Aquinas, often called the "Angelic Doctor."</note>, like Saint Augustine and Saint Thomas, employ.

Let these Gentlemen produce for me some passage that contains their own distinction between "religious" worship or "*non-religious*" worship. After that, I will tell them that if by this religious worship they mean a Divine Worship and of *latria*, that is due only to God, according to the Sentence: *You shall adore only one God. Matthew 4.* <note>original: "un seul Dieu tu adoreras." It is false what they charge against the Roman Church: that she puts the Virgin Mary in the place of God and Jesus Christ, and that she asks of her what God alone can grant. For the Church knows very well that there is a difference as vast as that between the infinite and the finite, and one can no more infer from the prayers the Church addresses to her that she can grant the graces that God alone can grant; because elsewhere the Church teaches in what sense she intends all those prayers—namely, that Mary obtains all these graces for us through her prayers, whereas we ask them of God as their Author and Giver.

In this, the Roman Church departs neither from the <term>Scripture</term> <note>The Bible.</note> nor from the pure and holy primitive Church. Not from Scripture, for we know well that no one can save us but God; nevertheless,

Certainly, you are not so lacking in discernment that you do not see here the error of your Gentlemen.

Admiring here the "good faith" of your Gentlemen <note>The author uses "Messieurs" (Gentlemen) to refer to the Protestant ministers he is debating.</note> in providing you such citations, I move forward. Since I did not put forward that proposition, I am surprised that these Gentlemen bestir themselves so much to make it seem unbelievable to you. They are creating their own oppositions according to their own whims when they do not know how to respond to mine—specifically, that *the Saints know our state and hear our prayers as much as it pleases God to reveal these to them for His glory and our good.* It was my actual propositions that they should have fought, and not those they made up themselves, of which I had not spoken.

In accordance with this, just as God revealed distant things and the thoughts of men's hearts to the living Prophets as much as it pleased Him, so now He reveals much more [to the Saints], <margin>1 Cor. 13:10</margin> since they *prophesy perfectly*. <note>A reference to 1 Corinthians 13:10: "But when that which is perfect is come, then that which is in part shall be done away." <meta>This page continues the author's defense of the invocation of saints. but as for the idea that the Fathers of the Old Testament for that reason knew nothing of the things of this world, I have proven the contrary to you, not through the Old Testament, but through the New. Abraham knew, says our Lord in the Gospel, the torments of the wicked rich man in hell <note>The author is citing the Parable of the Rich Man and Lazarus from Luke 16:19-31.</note>, the goods he had possessed in his life, the evils that Lazarus had suffered, that the brothers of this rich man had Moses and the Prophets, and that if they did not listen to them, they would not listen any more to someone from the dead who went to them; and Calvin <note>John Calvin (1509–1564), the French reformer. The author points out that even the Protestant leader accepts this biblical story as a factual account.</note> says that this is an account of a thing that actually happened. He knew, therefore, the things of this world by the revelation of God, although not so perfectly as now, when he sees God face to face.

<margin>From the lesser to the greater. <note>original: "A minori ad maius"</note></margin>
The production of these proofs has been a kind of argument that Philosophers hold as one of the strongest; it is *from the lesser to the greater*. For if the Saints were properly prayed to when they knew less of the things of this life, how much better can one pray to them now that they know more. This is the clarification I give you, and the best of all is the necessity you have to renounce the teachers who teach you to abandon the word of God and the universal practice of all Christendom for more than 16 centuries, in order to follow the heresy of a Vigilantius <note>Vigilantius (late 4th/early 5th century) was a priest who opposed the veneration of relics and saints; he was famously denounced by St. Jerome.</note> who, in the time of the Holy Fathers, used the same language against them as these Gentlemen use against the Catholic Church of this time, and who do not want to allow you to do the good that has been done in all ages of Christianity, and which was done even by a damned man in the flames of
